ParlaMint-HR_2013-01-30-0.u27999	Dear MPs and MPs, the applicable Roads Act, have been prescribed to classify public roads depending on their social, traffic and economic significance in highways, state roads, county and local roads. The proposed supplement regulates the jurisdiction of legal persons managing highways and national roads and the way works are done in the construction of certain public roads in case they are classified from highways to state roads and vice versa. The proposed way will allow for the continued implementation of investment projects, which will also prevent and eliminate possible major economic disturbances. In cases where a public road is classified on the highway, i.e. in a state road, is classified in another group in such a way that the highway passes into the state road and vice versa, and has already started with the construction of a particular section or at a high stage of construction, the legal person who managed that road will end up until the moment of classification. The technical characteristics of the road in which that road is classified should be taken into account. In the situation of reduced investment, this law will allow for the continuation of the start-up of the low-building construction sector. It is proposed to adopt the Law supplementing the Roads Act by emergency procedure in accordance with Article 159. Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament. The implementation of this Act does not require the provision of additional resources in the State budget of the Republic of Croatia. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2013-01-30-0.u28001	The Honorable President, the Honorable Deputy Minister with his associates, pardon himself, colleagues and fellow MPs. We have before us a proposal for a bill to supplement the Law on Roads. The road law regulates legal status, method of use, classification, planning and maintenance, management, protection and financing measures, concessions, public and unclassified roads. The addition of the Road Law will solve cases where public roads are classified on the highway, i.e. on the state road, are classified in another group in a way that passes the highway into the state road and vice versa, and have already begun to build a particular section or are in a high stage of construction. Initial work will be completed by a legal person who managed the road by the time the second group was classified. The technical characteristics of the road in which that road is classified should be taken into account. We can illustrate the application of this law’s supplement on a specific example. The residents of my County of Bjelovar-Bilogorska and the viritistic-subravska na once, i.e. the Koprivnica-križevci on the other direction of the bilogor-Podravska ipslon, this change is the only hope that this project will finally revive. At the end of November of last year, highways A12 and A13 were converted to state roads, DC10 and DC12, and Ipislon went into the jurisdiction of Croatian roads. In the opening explanation of this law, the proponent wrote the following "The Law on Roads has been signed to classify public roads depending on their social, transport and economic significance in highways, state roads, county and local roads. In cases where a public road classified on a highway or on a state road is classified in another group in such a way as to cross the highway into a state road and vice versa, and it has already begun to build a particular section or at a high stage of construction, legal persons who have managed that road by the time of the second group's classification will end." This is an expression in the explanation of the proposal of this law. The second thing I considered necessary was an emergency procedure as a reason why it was passed in this way and this law. It writes "in the proposed way it would allow for a smooth continuation and implementation of start-up investment projects, which would also prevent and eliminate possible major economic disturbances. For these reasons, it is also proposed to take effect on the day of its publication in the NN." These are two let's say the claims from this simple law with all one article. But before I go to that point, the essence of this law. I will remind you of everything that has happened in the past ten years since it started intensively in the construction of roads in the Republic of Croatia. All policies so far have said that the highway function is primarily traffic-integrated by the Republic of Croatia in all its parts and ensure the maximum level of security on all state and highways. The second task was faster flow of people, goods and services. And third not less important, for those public companies that invest, which create new values, ensure that a certain level of income can be built in order to finance anything that was built or wanted to be built in the next period. So last year, the finance minister said at the 4th month, I quote "the basic thing is that there is no further money in road construction. The traffic direction towards Ploč ends, everything else is invested in the maintenance of highways, state and regional roads." He also said "that neither the EU nor any other institutions support further investment in road construction in the Republic of Croatia". Those are the words of the Minister of Finance. To see the Deputy Prime Minister, Mr Branko Grcic recently passed this bill, saying, "We currently have 12 contracted projects worth 4.2 billion kuna, and because of the impasse in realisation, a 1.1 billion kuna has been lost. These projects are now defrosted and moving into realisation as soon as weather conditions are obtained. These projects have stopped mostly because of the problems of large construction companies but new contractors' associations are now involved with new project leaders, so I hope they will be completed this year." Comparing that with one China that opened 87,000 km of new highways last year alone, which in its system and on its surface of 9.7 million km2 has 4.1 million km of highways, it is clear that in the Republic of Croatia in the last year there was a stalemate, a total road stop. That's the first question. That may be the basic question when we're talking about sub-level ipsilon. If we turn the highway into a fast road, will we charge it and will the investor achieve a certain level of less invested money in the next decade as a result of the investment. We all know that only highways are charged in Croatia and income can be generated there. Everything else will be the cost. ParlaMint-HR_2013-01-30-0.u28269	Thank you, Mr. Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament. The government is giving you a proposal for a law on the electricity market. It is, of course, the adoption of the third energy directive package with which this law is harmonised and the standards it has adopted in its text. This proposal proposes to regulate the following issues: primary rules and measures to carry out energy activities and how electricity production, both from renewable energy sources and cogeneration plants, transmission, distribution, electricity, organisation of the electricity market, supply of electricity on the market, electricity trading, as well as the rights and obligations of the final buyer of electricity, access and separation of business books, financial reports of electricity operators, access to the network and of course the infringement provisions which are also in line with the requirements that standardise those Directives. What we specifically draw attention to, or, of course, to what we specifically refer to, to this law is the requirement to separate the network activity of electricity transmission from the production and supply of electricity that can be achieved by selecting one of the three models of construction, i.e. to restructure the separation of companies into two independent of which one TSO transmission system operator is, by dividing two companies by an independent system operator model when the system is operated by an undertaking that does not own an ISO network and by a separate independent transmission operator when the company remains unique but the company has a daughter of that company owned by a network and managed by an ITO model. The choice of models is decided by the owner of the company. For undertakings that were within a vertically integrated undertaking under the Directive on 3 September 2009 and engaged in the transmission of electricity, one of these three models is possible, while for new energy entities wishing to build a transmission network and become a transmission system operator, the TSI model is required. As regards HEP, a unique structure is considered according to the model of an independent transmission operator, i.e. an ITO model that is very similar to the model of separation to two companies, etc. The TSI model, with the ITO transfer operator remaining within the HEP group. In this proposal for a law, the conditions for implementing the model guaranteeing independence in the day-to-day business for which HERA is responsible are also proposed. The HERA responsible for the work of the Croatian Parliament must ensure that the conditions for the proper operation of the transmission system operator comply with the requirements of the Directive without changing the ownership structure of HEP. The basic assets of the Croatian transmission system operator as it will be called in the future must be clearly separated from other parts of the HEP group, and these are high-level networks in the future when they are citizens of the European so-called. The electrical highways of the highest voltages. On HEP d.d. it is clear that... the means over which the transfer is carried out and from those belonging to the distribution. As regards an operator of a distribution system operating at lower voltage levels, its underlying assets do not have to be allocated from the HEP group, but in the day-to-day business businesses must have business independence from other activities within the group. In the event that it is established that its position in the HEP group prevents the development of the market, the Government may decide to allocate the basic assets of the distribution system operator in the manner provided for in the transfer. It is important to stress that all procedures must be clear, they must be transparent. Since 2014, a single electricity market has been established in the Union where all participants carrying out the same type of electricity activity have the same rights and responsibilities, and the European Union bodies, the European Energy Market Regulator ACER and the European Commission are permanently monitoring that market. The law further proposes the adoption of 10-year and 3-year development plans and 1-year investment plans of transmission and distribution system operators approved by HERA. In accordance with the development, maintenance and work plans, the HERA network shall bring prices for network connection and for the use of transmission and distribution networks. So far, HERA has brought only methodologies and given an opinion on the proposal for the amount of prices, and the Government of the Republic of Croatia has brought them. Electricity supply, also market activity, except for the supply provided as a public service, which is universal supply and guaranteed supply. The right to universal supply is provided by customers of the age category of household unless they want to participate in the market and choose their supplier freely. The right to guaranteed supply shall be granted by all buyers if their market supplier ceases to work. The way in which universal services and guaranteed services are provided shall be determined by the methodologies made by HERA, and the Government shall determine by its decision the electricity entities which determine the obligation to provide the electricity supply service to be provided as universal or guaranteed. Until a decision is made by the Government, the customer supplier who has the right to universal or guaranteed supply continues to provide the service. In particular, the quality of electricity supply is regulated. HER is the one that lays down the conditions or rules on the quality of electricity supply. Furthermore, the obligations of suppliers to end customers are specified in detail. Provisions shall be introduced on purchasers of the tariff category of household under special protection under universal supply, regional cooperation areas shall also be defined, or regional cooperation in the field of electricity shall be promoted and infringement provisions or sanctions shall be prescribed for those participants who violate regulations or also endanger competition in the electricity sector. It's final. This proposal for a law analoguely implements all the requirements of the third energy package, which refers to how the procedures for the construction of new production capacity through the approval system, the exceptional implementation of the public tender, the insurance of the development of the construction of the electricity network, the criteria and procedures for accessing and the use of the transmission network or distribution network, the surveillance of security of supply, the technical rules of the electrical energy system, the separation of transmission activities from the production and supply of electricity, and the tasks of the transmission system operator, the separation and operation of the distribution system operator, additional powers and responsibilities of HER in this area, and the legal position and protection of electricity customers. I would like to thank you for your attention and thank you for the debate that has been held on the working bodies, i.e. the parliamentary committees. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2013-01-30-0.u28275	Dear Vice-President of the Parliament, the esteemed deputy minister with a co-worker, respected colleagues and colleagues. Here is what the Deputy Minister and Deputy Minister pointed out to me, as necessary, the legal framework of the Croatian energy sector, and in short terms, we need to comply with the EU acquis. This is, of course, also related to the EU self-evaluation which assessed the functioning of the electricity market in Croatia as non-transparent and in good part not marketable and therefore Croatia with the closure of Chapter 15 in pre-accession negotiations obliged to implement a new energy package within the deadlines within which other EU members must do so. This is, of course, one of the objectives, and other objectives are to ensure safe and quality energy supply, development of the electricity market, competitiveness of prices, consumer protection, efficient production and use of energy and of course environmental protection. A few months ago, we passed the Energy Act as a roof law for the entire energy sector, and this law sets out both the frameworks and the clear rules relating to the electricity market. It is also clear that individual legal institutes and proposed legal solutions represent quite a lot of newspapers in the organisation and functioning of the electricity sector, both for energy entities and for competent institutions, both ultimately and for customers. But the interests of the Republic of Croatia in this area are clear. This is primarily to ensure sufficient production and supply of electricity for both citizens and for business and development of economic operators. And what's of course most important at real prices. What this law sets is important issues related to the future organisation and development of the market and the relationship of participants in the electricity market. In fact, the EU electricity market should function in 2014 when it will be free to choose between electricity suppliers in the EU. Which, of course, opens up the possibility that EU suppliers can buy energy on the Croatian market. So, the competitive electricity price on the one hand and the secure and reliable supply of electricity on the other hand is Croatia's main interests, and in order to achieve them, we need to adapt to legislation, but in fact, the most important business and way of doing our electricity sector. This law further lays down very important issues of public service obligations. It is clear from the public service that there is an obligation to provide public electricity supply services as a universal service, which ensures the right of customers in the category of households to provide electricity to the prescribed quality as well as the right to an equal price throughout the territory of the Republic of Croatia. Thus, it protects the right customer in cases where it cannot affect the circumstances and when it loses supply. This may occur where the supplier is no longer able to supply the final customer in accordance with the supply contract due to possible bankruptcy, insolvency, electricity market disturbances, and these are all real risks posed by the open market. This law also ensures the protection of vulnerable customers and prevents the appearance of so-called. energy poverty. The threatened customers shall be entitled to supply in accordance with the decision of the competent social care authority. Because of the price of energy, our citizens have found themselves under a great deal of impact and something out of objective circumstances that we as a state cannot influence, and that is simply the fact that the prices of energy in the world are growing but mostly because of an irresponsible policy that has not been dealt with in any way in all these years and tried to solve it. And precisely because of this blow to our citizens, this law provides for the protection of our most endangered citizens. It is also important to note here that public service must be provided in an impartial, transparent and easily verifiable manner. The Government of the Republic of Croatia is obliged to inform the European Commission of any measures taken with regard to public service obligations including the protection of final customers. HERA, on the other hand, is obliged to carry out consultations with representatives of consumer protection organisations regarding the decision on the amount of tariff items, i.e. the price for individual electricity activities carried out as a public service. In addition to the universal service, there is a guaranteed supply of electricity, and it supplies the final buyer with electricity without a specific demand from the final customer in the event of the outage of its supplier from the electricity market if the final buyer cannot affect the circumstances and thus loses its supply without protection. The procedure for the exit of the problem supplier from the electricity market, regardless of the form of its exit, is laid down by the rules of the organisation of electricity markets. The second part that regulates this law is market activities involving electricity production, electricity trade and end-of-life supply. They are based on rules governing market relations where the quantities and prices of electricity supplied are freely negotiated or negotiated. The objectives are, of course, to meet the needs of customers but also to achieve a safe, reliable and quality supply of electricity, while respecting the principle of competition and the equal position of all market operators. It is important to underline that the law clearly determines that distribution and transmission of electricity remain regulated activities, supply and production of electricity become market activities. And here we come to a key point, which is that electricity will not be purchased from power plants that are not competitive but will look for cheaper electricity sources in the EU market while the transmission of electricity will remain under control and control of the state. And of course we are coming up with the inevitable issue of restructuring HEP. The law states that the company will be able to decide itself on the organisational model of the organisation of the transmission system operator. The chosen HEP model proposes how it plans to organise its activities and calls for the certification of HERA, which will require the approval of the European Commission for this model. In the case of a positive response from the European Commission, HERA shall decide and actively monitor the restructuring process which must be completed within 12 months of the date of adoption of this law. Yesterday, on the Committee on Economy, we had the opportunity to hear a lot of complaints about the choice of models, which I have to admit was quite confusing with the fact that this process was due to begin in 2009. So the HDZ government could decide for whatever model it wanted, could apply it, so they had two years to choose a model and to restructure HEP. But as usual, they did nothing. And here may be more about deadlines, the importance of deadlines in preparing this debate for the law I have come to the conclusion that the European Commission is very much monitoring and sanctioning these deadlines and applying high penalties, so here are our neighbours, Slovenians, already paying 25 thousand euros per day under the directive due to delays, and Poland has been paying as much as 80,000 euros. In conclusion, this law will create conditions for safe and quality electricity supply, ensure market development and price competitiveness but what is most important will ensure customer protection and therefore the HNS Club will support this law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2013-01-30-0.u28293	esteemed colleagues and colleagues, distinguished representatives of the government. At the very beginning of the presentation on the final draft of the Electricity Market Act on behalf of the SDP Club, I would like to say that the SDP Club will support the adoption of this law, which brings domestic legislation into line with EU directives. 3. A European energy package. Unfortunately, we are doing this alignment with a great delay because the Republic of Croatia promised to bring domestic legislation into line with 3 in the time of the temporary closure of the negotiating chapter 15, which covers energy. The European Energy Package until March 2011. So we're almost two years late and in these two years we've done nothing sensational to justify this delay. It is fortunate that because of this delay, we do not have to pay for the penalty, but the deadlines in which we will pay for the penalty unless we make visible shifts in terms of full implementation of the energy directives, which are not financially harmless to us at all, is approaching us. In that sense, at the SDP club, we are convinced that it would be extremely irresponsible not to support the adoption of this law even though with its contents we are not always fully satisfied. Why do we have certain reserves according to the text of this final bill? The text of this law is very complicated we would say impervious even for lawyers who do not also possess certain knowledge from the field of electricity, and the laws should be understandable not only to lawyers but also to the average educated citizens and citizens of Croatia. The complexity of the law stems not only from the fairly mechanical takeover of the EU's content, but from a rather incomprehensible fact that the proposer of the laws of the Ministry of Economy decided in the final proposal of the law to keep all three proposed models from the EU directive, or that the ministry did not clearly decide for the ITO, ISO or TSO model, to separate the transfer from the vertically integrated company. The ministry suffered from all three models, which undoubtedly caused no problems with the team who wrote the law because the final decision to choose for Croatia the most acceptable model of the law had to provide for three different scenarios of the future Croatian electricity market. Unfortunately, due to the complexity of the problem, there have been some discrepancies in certain articles of the law that must be antipicated or envisioned by all three variants, because in certain provisions of the law, the choice of the TSI is predicted in the opposite of the notices of the proposed person, rather than the ITO model that the HEP favours. Where's that visible? This is evident in the transitional and final provisions of the draft law, in particular in Article 76(6), which gives the impression that the state has decided on a TSI model rather than on an ITO model that all colleagues and colleagues who listened and listened to the presentation of representatives and representatives of the ministry on boards have had the opportunity to learn about the selected model. In view of the fact that the law allows for the selection of any of the three models of the HEP OPS to be singled out, why is this article and the entry anti-nomination in the company HOPS d.o.o. is then it actually means that the selected model TSO does not ISO or ITO. Otherwise, an independent Croatian independent ISO transmission system operator or an independent ITO transmission operator should be written in the name. Given the fact that this debate is being heard, the question is, and is seen by citizens and citizens of Croatia who in energy 3. The European energy package and the proposed European models know probably much less than I do, and I am certainly not an energy-educated person, I am sure they are absolutely incomprehensible to ITO, ISO, TSO model and what implications the Croatian electricity system will have to choose which of the models offered. ITO, ISTO and TSO models are ways in which EU Member States can separate network activities, hence the transmission of electricity from the production of electricity. The reason why the EU insists that European countries separate the transmission from production by abolishing the monopoly of national energy companies that, in the vast majority of European countries, prevent the liberalisation of the electricity market as they protect their privileged monopolies. The separation of network activities from production is a key step leading to the introduction of market principles in 2015 of the common electricity market in Europe because it allows all producers of electricity under the same conditions at the same price without obstruction and discrimination to make access to network activities currently controlled by HEP in Croatia. This is particularly important for renewable energy producers across Europe to meet the obstructions of national energy companies that did not want new players to take a little bit at a time, considered and considered their cookies. The EU through 3. The European Energy Package allows Member States to enter through the selection of one of the three models offered by separating networks from production activities. Therefore, the interest of the professional public in the proposal of the Ministry of Economy expected that the ministry as a representative of the state, and only the state could determine the strategic interest and choose as a roof state strategy for the model that is best for Croatia, clearly determine and select one of the three models offered. Unfortunately, the ministry did not do this, but decided from us a completely unclear reason to leave the choice of the model to HEP therefore to a company whose monopoly is implemented 3. The European Energy Package aims to contain or completely break. However, unless the HEP manages in time to detach the transmission system operator from HEP under any model of sanctions, it will bear the Republic of Croatia, not HEP, and this will be the political responsibility of the Minister of Economy and MING, not HEP and Mr Koracevic. It is known to the professional public that HEP favours the ITO model, which is absolutely understandable from the position of the company's interest, because with the help of this model HEP will preserve the greatest impact on network activities or their control. Why? The ITO model is an independent transmission operator model that implies that HEP amends, within the time limit set by this law, a declaration of the establishment of HEP OPS, the recapitalisation by entering into the underlying capital of things and rights relating to the transmission network and ensures other conditions of independence which it oversees and approves HERA as a regulator. In other words, with this model, the transmission operator remains under the hat of the HEP group, the model is the most administratively complicated and requires constant control of the regulator for which we have reasonable doubts that HERA will be unable to implement because of all other tasks it has under the Energy Act and the HERA Act. This model is the only successful of all the members who have chosen the ITO model implemented Austria with great difficulty, although it is a very well-organised system and a country which Croatia is not yet unfortunately. Other countries that have opted for the ITO model have major implementation problems, and some are already paying for foams like Bulgaria today. The second model is the TSI model, i.e. the ownership separation model of the transmission system operator which implies the following. Within the deadline provided for by this law, HEP shall implement the recapitalisation, introduce HEP OPS matters and rights related to the transmission network. The second step is to move the stake in HEP OPS to the Republic of Croatia. The new and sole owner of the transmission operator of the Republic of Croatia changes the company's company to the HOPS, the Croatian transmission system operator and, within the time limit provided for by HEP and the transmission system operator, the contract to assume the obligations of HEP d.d. in relation to the transmission network. This model has been selected by a large number of members of the European Union and has so far proved to be the best in practice. What is crucial for this model is that the state, through a new company, has its natural monopoly in its possession and removes the possibility of privatisation and thus separates the transport system of gas from the INA, which has proved to be extremely successful. In the gas business, Croatia has applied a TSO model that has proved successful, we have experience in its implementation, and there is a legitimate question why we do not apply the same successful model to the electricity market. This is, according to our court, a model that best safeguards the public interest of the Republic of Croatia. The third model is the ISO model, i.e. the model of an independent system transmission operator, which implies that HEP is established by the HEP within the time limit provided for by law by the HEP transmission network and that things and rights related to the transmission network are introduced into the underlying capital. In the second step, the HEP stake in HEP OPS moves to the Republic of Croatia after which the new and only owner of the Republic of Croatia changes the company to an independent transmission system operator. Within the time limit provided for in this Act, HEP and HEP transmission networks shall enter into a transfer agreement for HEP d.d. in relation to the transmission network, and HERA monitors and approves the commercial and financial relations of HEP d.d. and HEP transmission network d.o.o. In this model, the operator remains under the control of HEP and the control of the non-discriminatory principle of access to the network is carried out by HERA as a regulator. This model is in Europe, as far as I know, implemented by the only Greece, has a lot of problems, and it seems to me that the current situation is so prepared to privatise network activities. The choice of the model is extremely important because it depends on whether Croatia will retain control of a strategic natural monopoly called the transmission of electricity in the future. For example, if the transfer remains within the HEP group and one day a future government under EU pressure or for some other reason decides to privatise HEP, we can easily lose a strategic natural monopoly, which would be an irreparable loss and almost betray national interests. We must not forget that the transmission network was built with their means by generations of Croatian citizens and citizens and that it is a capital that we simply cannot gamble with. It must also be of key interest to the State to allow equal access to the transmission network to all entities, whether it is HEP or private domestic or foreign investors to enter energy projects, to develop the electricity market, because meeting the energy needs of the population is one of the key strategic obligations of the state. In this regard, we appeal to the Government of the Republic of Croatia to demonstrate the maximum seriousness and long-term protection of the strategic energy interests of the Republic of Croatia. Finally, we warn of two other illogical provisions in the law, Article 11(8), which is not clear why the first purchase of renewable electricity is introduced for a period of 25 years, because the incentives last 14 years, and given the fact that the electricity market is being introduced, this provision constitutes a distortion of competition and it would be wise for the Government to correct it by its amendment to this proposal of the law. In addition, in Article 77(1) for the supply of final customers in the household category, HEP ODS is currently in charge. The new law does not allow it to supply customers, so it is necessary to set the time limit to which it is allowed, i.e. when the Government of the Republic of Croatia will decide as set out in Article 5(5). Thank you very much.

The first working group meeting took place on June 6th and from that first meeting until September 12th when the last working group meeting was held, including HEP, gave me as much as I think about 300 objections to a specific draft of the law. On 12 September, when the last meeting of the Working Group for the Construction of Electricity Markets Act was held, a draft law on the electricity market was presented in which only one article regulated that the transmission system operator should be organised according to the ITO model, maintaining the maintenance of the entire HEP as such, and that any change to ownership separation would be carried out in the event of an unsatisfactory implementation of the ITO model through the amendment of the law itself. This solution was unanimously accepted by the working group, and was agreed by the representatives of the economy ministry. The measures provided for in this Regulation are in accordance with the opinion of the Standing Committee on Plants, Animals, Food and Feed, A draft law on the electricity market that norms the possibility of the ownership of the HEP distribution system operator. However, the proposal for a law on the electricity market published on the website of the Ministry of Economy had nothing to do with the text established by the working group of the Ministry of Economy for the construction of that law. Decisions that standardise the models of the separation and separation of transmission system operators, which are those of the 14-18th and distribution system operator in Article 36, are contrary to the conclusion of the working group from 12 September 2012 and as such even worsened to the damage to HEP compared to that version previously presented to the working group. It is important to note that hep, INA, PLINACRO, HERA, HROTE, HANDA and the Chief State Inspectorate worked in the task force alongside experts from the Ministry of Economy. As for Articles 14 and 18, all three possible models are regulated according to that European Union directive, just as well as my colleague Popiac says, and we simply do not know which model to be applied, regardless of how the representatives of HEP are fighting for the ITO model. Although there is no obligation to own the OPS in Article 14, the application of the equity factor as a rule is normised and the other models can be applied exceptionally. The more the term can be organized, the more the term is organized, which does not correspond to the actual state or to the expected future solution. So the claims made in the bill as such are false, so I would only beg for an indication about it. Article 36 of the Law provides for the possibility of the ownership of the distribution system operator without the prior consent of the Croatian Parliament, even though Article 26 of Directive 2009/72EU says: "These rules do not create an obligation for the ownership of the underlying assets of the distribution system operator from a vertically integrated entity. In all this, we could easily conclude that someone really is in a hurry to meet investors in this article 36th, and even the fourth package will not require this separation of the distribution system in the ownership structure, but only the separation of the system in the organisational and management structure. We are left with an answer why the privatisation of energy companies that are the strategic and security issue of each country is covered by the entry and harmonisation of the law with the EU is so easy and here is the explanation. We have heard several times today that the application of such laws will bring a lot of unknowns, a lot of problems for the simple reason that HEP as such is not ready for such a competition. Today's emphasis on energy supply should and supply should certainly be placed only on supply and as such on environmental protection. The priority should be less on market liberalisation and more on security. That's what I think Croatian citizens are looking for and they'd be glad to get it. Privatisation in the energy sector should follow Croatia's line and degree of integration into the EU in order never to put the market in the hands and will of private, especially foreign capital. We have heard from many exhibitors today how important energy is to us, we have heard what it means to each and every one of us individually, but also to the economy as a whole. We've heard just as much as haste with certain decisions wouldn't be good and here's really once again as many of my predecessors I appeal to think about whether we have time to discuss this in this House once more constructively and more thoroughly than we've done today.

ParlaMint-HR_2013-01-30-0.u28441	Dear President-in-Office of the Parliament, the distinguished deputy minister with a co-worker, co-workers and colleagues. Here is a really important law that will finally create preconditions for the purpose and effective management of mineral raw materials. Personally, I believe that this law corrects and removes certain indetermination, indifference and problems arising from the concrete implementation of the applicable law. Some of them were also pointed out by the European Commission, as well as by certain mining operators, who, in a particularly challenging situation, have, in view of the economic crisis which, by blocking construction activities, have been directly mirrored by the mining activity. The Mining Act has been developed in order to bring the legislative framework of the Republic of Croatia into line with the acquis of the European Union in the field of energy and industry. But I would point out that it was actually built with a basic intention and purpose to ensure fair market conditions in which the Republic of Croatia as the owner of mineral raw materials and mining economic operators as concessionaries for the exploitation of mineral raw materials found their interest in the development of mining activities in Croatia. I would refer specifically to several points in this debate which actually introduce important changes in relation to the existing law. The first part refers to the tender procedure. This law drastically simplifys the procedure, but what is most important is that it allows the concession to be given to the one who invested or invested in the research. Because so far, the situation has looked like this: the company has gone into research and invested significant resources in it. Maybe here's just a orderly amount of research costs in solid raw materials, so stone, clay, metal can't be made for less than 5 million kuna. And when we talk about exploring oil and gas for one well, it's about 20 million kuna, with no serious research you can do without a minimum of three wells. So this is about really large funds invested in research so that the company that invested those funds in the research would be put in a position to go to the competition because in the tender the right to exploit gets a company that has not invested either. This, of course, puts all potential investors in a situation where they are not ready to take a huge risk, so in the area of exploitation, there is one status quo in Croatia for a long time. The new law in this section prescribes, instead of the three competitions so far, only one public tender for the approval of research and raw materials for the award of a concession for the exploitation of mineral raw materials. Therefore, with respect to all legal obligations, the procedure is accelerated and 100% legal certainty is given to mining entities that have done research. Furthermore, the law stipulates that stopping for more than 180 days means actually breaking up. The practice has shown that large systems, therefore large companies and most often sides that have the right to exploit, for example 5 fields, but in 4 they have not exploited but have only done it in one field. Thus, they agreed to a monopoly that raw materials can only be obtained in one field at the price they set and in the time period they set. And that, of course, with the fact that the cost had only one exploitation field, so costs and machinery and labour force. Such a legal provision prevents this kind of manipulation. A significant newspaper in this law is also a new way of calculating concessionaires' fees. The law defines exactly at which point the concessionary starts to pay compensation, and the fee itself now consists of two parts of a fiscal charge that is charged on the surface of the exploitation field and as such goes to local self-government units, which is particularly supportive because it is absolutely logical that units of local self-government where research and exploitation are done to them and go to them. The second variable part, which is calculated as a fee on the value of the quantity and the income is evenly distributed between municipalities and cities, the county and the state. So two-thirds of it still remain for local communities. What is also particularly important for the prominent is the control of the fee. In fact, the law correctly regulates that every year 31.12., concessionaries must provide a map of the situation of mining works, where it is seen exactly what has been done and done over the past year. Also, there is an obligation to make elaborates, reserves every 5 years. This gives the economy's competent authority a clear insight into the scale of exploitation. Changes are also related to spatial planning issues. Research can't begin unless it's foreseen by a spatial plan. This is also an important instrument of legal protection for economic operators, as this does not lead them to explore, or invest, as I have previously said significant funds in an area that may eventually never get a location permit, and thus also a work permit. In the case of the scope of the exploitation field, the most important moment to be regulated by this law is the identification of all possible obstacles that exist in a given area. So protected zones, archaeological sites, etc. where no exploitation will be possible, and these areas are immediately excluded from the field of exploitation. So the economic operators are doing all the necessary documentation and all the necessary actions only for the part of the exploitation field that will be able to exploit and not therefore for the whole area as it has been the case so far. One of the perhaps most significant provisions of this Law is certainly the one relating to the recovery of the exploitation field. Unfortunately, frequent examples from our cities and municipalities have been that the company has been exploiting a specific area for years in accordance with all regulations. But when the exploitation ends, it avoids the recovery of that space in every possible way. The most common model for the company to be put into bankruptcy after the completion of the exploitation and thus avoid the obligation of recovery. In order to avoid such and similar situations, this Law also introduces a new model whereby any economic operator carrying out the exploitation must give the State a bank guarantee in the amount of the environmental study assessment. This provides the state with funds to resuscitate the locality regardless of the fate of the company following the completion of the exploitation. I find this a truly important change that guarantees the preservation of our country's environment above all. I would even point out in particular that this is most important for new potential findings in the Adriatic Sea because we, of course, must provide research and exploitation of our natural resources in the Adriatic Sea, and on the other hand we must ensure absolutely possible protection of our Adriatic. This is also related to the provision for increasing sanctions for both economic operators and responsible persons, and we believe that these increased penalties will primarily prevent potential offenders, and there is also the obligation to compensate for both legal and natural persons. These are all instruments that protect the state from illegal availability of natural resources. Furthermore, the course of exploitation is limited to 40 years, not as it was now, as someone said to the country's centre and to the end of the world. All existing concessionaries will have two years to adapt to the new law. The rights acquired are of course recognised, but for the sake of protection and competition and of course national natural resources, this adjustment is necessary. Here and perhaps, in conclusion, this law makes a number of positive changes relating to ensuring competition and competitiveness. Legal protection and safety of economic operators and investors are ensured, but on the other hand, protection and proper treatment of the natural resources of our country are ensured. Of course, the HNS supports this law, although two discussions have been held for a month and how many I have sent as many as 8 working group meetings felt the need for another reading. We support it, of course, it's about the very important natural resources of our country and I believe that in these two readings we will be able to find the best solution and the best model for this Mining Act. Thank you.

This proposal for a mining law regulates the management of mineral raw materials and the planning of mining economic activities, research and identification of reserves of mineral raw materials, the production and verification of mining projects, the exploitation of mineral raw materials, the construction and use of mining facilities and plants. The creation and verification of an idea mining project which becomes the basis for the assessment of the acceptability of the environmental network procedures, environmental impact studies and for the establishment of a mining operation permit, the limited duration of the exploitation period to 40 years at the end of which the recovery of space is committed, of course, if the concession is not extended in the final draft of the law. The rules for specific conditions of exploitation in terms of the determination of reserves of mineral raw materials and their exploitation in relation to determined quantities and regulations are also adapted to other specific cases such as storage of gases and the permanent disposal of CO2 gases in geological structures. This law lays down provisions governing the storage and permanent disposal of those gases. ParlaMint-HR_2013-01-30-0.u28491	Thank you, Mr. President, distinguished MPs and representatives in the Croatian Parliament. Here, after more than 10 years of application of the Defence Act, which has experienced self-defined novels, the Government of the Republic of Croatia comes before this House of Commons with a new Proposal for a Law on Defence. While the current Defence Act came after the constitutional changes in 2000 and 2001, which marked the abandonment of the semi-presidential model of power, it was in fact a kind of compromise between constitutional holders of the defence function by the new legislative proposal to move towards a new modern defence concept and its conceptual and institutional positioning through several key directions. In the new bill, the defence is considered both as a state function and as an area of action of different holders, not as before as it was a defence system. The new geopolitical reality and the international position of the Republic of Croatia are also respected, and the defence is seen in the context of NATO membership, and we believe in the European Union soon. There are assumptions that the state is functioning within the concept of collective defence. In the area of defence management, simpler and more consistent models are established, clearer division of competences, avoidance of their overlap with moderate movement towards the parliamentary system, of course, while respecting the constitutional position of the President of the Republic as Supreme Commander. It also fully regulates the activities of the Armed Forces in the new circumstances of the application of Article 7 of the new Constitution of the Republic of Croatia in 2010, and membership in the NATO Alliance and the future in the European Union, and prescribes their action outside the borders of the country in peace support operations, crisis response operations, humanitarian missions, and for the first time regulates the delegation of powers of command and control over the commands of international forces to international forces, the new staff structure and their status are verified. As regards the Armed Forces, in addition to their fundamental role in the protection of sovereignty and independence and the defence of its territorial integrity of the Republic of Croatia, the armed forces' tasks remain to participate in peace support operations, crisis response operations, humanitarian missions and other activities abroad. Then providing assistance in defences to States allies in the event of an armed attack on one or more of them in accordance with concluded international agreements. Participation also in the EU's joint efforts in the spirit of solidarity if one member is exposed to a terrorist attack or becomes a victim of natural and human activity caused by disaster. And fourthly, it is aid to the institutions of civil authorities, protection and rescue organisations, and to the population in the event of disasters, major accidents and accidents in search and rescue, as well as the transport of injured and ill. The proposed legal text also regulates the jurisdiction of the Croatian Parliament, the President of the Republic, the Government of the Republic of Croatia, the Ministry of Defence and the General Staff in the area of defence. The Croatian Parliament, as it has decided so far, has decided on war and peace, and has exercised civil oversight over the Armed Forces. It decides on the amount and structure of the funds needed to finance the defence through the state budget, adopts a long-term development plan of the Armed Forces, which I believe will also be in this High House by the end of this year as well as an annual defence report and brings forward a defence strategy and identifies foreign direct threats. It also takes a decision to refer recruits to military service, to decide on the activities of the Armed Forces outside the borders of the Republic of Croatia through various modalities which are recognised in the new draft law, and to take the decision to refer requests for assistance to the defence of the Republic of Croatia to the allies. The President of the Republic under the Constitution is the Supreme Commander of the Armed Forces, and his responsibilities are also prescribed by this law. As you unfortunately had opportunities to read in the media at the end of last year, inscriptions appeared on how this bill to the President of the Republic took some of the powers and powers of him. However, we are responsible for stressing that the proposed law prescribes and elaborated powers of the president in line with the amendments to the Constitution and the changes that have taken place in the course of reforms and the transformation of the Armed Forces. The paper was introduced that the President of the Republic appointed military commanders on the duty of brigade and general, i.e. the Admiral, and this change is reflected in the fact that now it is the military commanders because the Constitution itself says that the President of the Republic has appointed and resolved military commanders in accordance with the Constitution and that the law has adapted to that category so that it has explicitly defined who are military commanders, and not as it has been so far to appoint all officers in the orderly places of the Brigadier or General and Admiral. The President of the Republic also received the authority to prescribe the peacetime and military structure of the Staff, the Command and the Armed Forces units, including the formation of the Chief Staff of the Armed Forces. The proposed law also prescribes the jurisdiction of the executive authority, i.e. Governments of the Republic of Croatia in the area of defence against the submission of the proposal to the Croatian Parliament, the annual defence reports and other acts to the adoption of a defence plan, the strategic defence review, the exit of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Croatia outside the border and the entry of the Armed Forces of the Allied States into the Republic of Croatia when it comes to the provision of humanitarian aid, and the crossing of the border of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Croatia and the entry of the Armed Forces of other countries when it comes to military exercises. The Ministry of Defence as a central body has planned, directing, coordination and control functions in defence and provides conditions for the operation and operation of the Armed Forces. The main headquarters as a joint body of the Armed Forces is responsible for the preparation, command, use and use of the Armed Forces. At the head of the Chief Staff, he remains the chief of staff, command and troops and remains chief military adviser to both the President of the Republic and the Minister of Defence. The paper was introduced in part of the term of office of Chief of Staff. So far, the term of office has been put in place for five years now that the term of office of Chief of Staff is four years, with the possibility of extending for a year under the same conditions as its appointment. The paper also presents that for the first time in the Bill's proposal in defence, it is prescribed that the Chief of Staff shall have full command in the Armed Forces on the basis of orders and orders from the President of the Republic and decisions of the Minister of Defence. In the course of the allocation of troops to members of the Armed Forces, the Chief of Staff may transfer the power of operational and tactical command and supervision to the competent international or national command and commanders. The Defence Council shall remain a form of coordination of the body for the coordination of positions and conclusion and recommendations in the area of defence. As far as the rights and obligations of the citizens of the Republic of Croatia in defence are concerned, they have a duty and a right to participate in defence through military obligation, voluntary military training, service to the contractual reserve, have a labour obligation, work assistance and a material obligation. The law elaborated a constitutional provision under which the military obligation and defence of the Republic of Croatia is the duty of all capable citizens. Military service, or civil service, is mandatory under the law in the event of direct threat or wartime, while the Croatian Parliament can decide on the introduction of a military service obligation, or civil service. In this bill, we have divided the reserve of the Armed Forces into a contractual reserve and a mobilization reserve. And mobilization to something we call a non-classified and classified reserve. Not to enter the details of the basic difference between classified and non-classified reserves is the fact that the classy reserve is the one who either served military time or was on voluntary military training or was a member of the active group of the Armed Forces. This means that it has the necessary skills and military skills to be able to serve in the reserve while the unclassified reserve is a person who unfortunately has none but what is required in carrying out a military obligation, which is just a entry into military records. As far as the contractor's concerned, it's a new category we've introduced. It is a category of which we will seek the meaning of persons who are in the reserve, who have the necessary knowledge and skills, or individual professions that are lacking in the Armed Forces, will try to make a contract with them in such a way that they will be paid during their contractual obligation, and in return they are obliged to participate in preparations for their training under a military call in order to be used by the Armed Forces as necessary. So either in peace-related situations in crisis response operations or in other situations necessary for the Armed Forces. Voluntary military training is actually a name for voluntary military service so far and is carried out when a military service obligation is not introduced. Voluntary military training can be addressed by the end of the calendar year in which 26 years of life is spent by the Croatian citizens who apply to the Ministry of Defence. Women can also benefit from voluntary military training. And voluntary military training meets one of the preconditions for joining the active military service that will later be the subject of my speech when I speak of the Law of Service in the Armed Forces. The labour obligation is the obligation of employees in state authorities, local and regional self-government units and legal persons who are subject to the obligation of defence preparation. The paper is also an obligation to assist legal persons and citizens where labour assistance can be imposed for the purpose of carrying out the duties of defence-holders in the event of war and the situation of direct endangerment. In the state of war and the state of direct endangerment of the legal person, the citizens of the Republic of Croatia also have a material obligation. As regards the current terms of the peace operation and the missions that have been implemented in our legislation, we are leaving this term and introducing a new one – peace support operations in accordance with NATO standards, and we define that these are operations using diplomatic, civil and military means to establish or maintain peace, and such operations may include conflict prevention, establishment and maintenance of peace, impose and build peace. The use of the Armed Forces is decided by the Croatian Parliament, the President of the Republic, the Prime Minister and the defence minister. In the law, you may notice that the concept of command to the Armed Forces is no longer applied to such a large extent, but the whole law requires decisions on the use of the Armed Forces because, over these 10 years, the application of the law has shown that command is in the rule of law when the time of the war is when the President of the Republic directly commands the Armed Forces while the command in peace that has been carried out through the Defence Minister in this way has been transformed into making decisions as to how the Armed Forces are used. The President of the Republic shall order the use and command of the Armed Forces in a state of war and shall be responsible for defending the independence and territorial integrity of the Republic of Croatia. In the event of a situation of immediate threat, the President of the Republic may, with the subsignation of the Prime Minister, order the use of the armed forces even though no war is declared. The use of armed forces in situations that require combating aircraft or vessels that violate the sovereignty of the Republic of Croatia and national security will be governed by a special law. The application of the Defence Act did not know the term of use of the armed forces, but was recognised by the constitutional changes of 2010. So the proposed Law on the Use of Armed Forces is decided by the Government or the Defence Minister, and such decisions are implemented through the command of the Chief Staff. The decision on the use of armed forces in the event of disasters is taken by the Government, and in the event of major accidents and accidents, the defence minister shall, on the basis of the request of the body responsible for the protection and rescue. Armed forces may also, as I said, be used to assist in fire protection and search if the fire protection authorities, i.e. search and rescue do not have adequate means and capabilities. The armed forces can also be used to assist in monitoring and protecting the rights and interests of the Republic of Croatia at sea through the Coast Guard. Armed forces can also provide assistance to the local community and civil society in the implementation of humanitarian and general interest projects under the bill. Certain units or dedicated organised forces of the armed forces may be declared as multinational response forces organised within the NATO Alliance or as part of a common security and defence policy. On the day of entry into force of the new Defence Act, the Law on the participation of members of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Croatia, the police, civil protection and civil servants and subsistence officers in peace operations and other activities abroad shall cease to apply, in a section relating to members of the armed forces, so that this matter will only be governed by the proposed law. The proposed law also regulates the area of defence preparations. Civilians are Government, Ministry, County and City of Zagreb. Legal persons of particular importance for defence shall be determined by the Government and shall be obliged to participate in defence preparations in accordance with the implementation of the plans of the action of civil defence preparations holders. The law prescribes the jurisdiction of the defence inspectorate, and also provides for military police to be established in the ministry of defence and armed forces with the task of carrying out military-police operations. The law further regulates the area of military, air and maritime transport, as well as military construction and spatial planning in military locations. The Defence Act is also proposed to regulate the area of material care and defence financing. For the implementation of the law, funds shall be provided in the state budget at the position of the Ministry of Defence. The government of the Republic of Croatia proposes this law through two readings, convinced that it is the only possible way to pass a quality Defence Act. We would like to hear the proposals and suggestions of all those involved in their adoption in order to draw up and establish a final draft of the law after the debate and analysis has been carried out. The proposed changes in the legal framework will allow for a new momentum in the transformation of defence and the development of professional and effective armed forces. As for the Law of Service in the Armed Forces, it is a logical continuation of the changes that took place in the Defence Act. So we bring him in a package like it was in 2002. And we have amended certain legal solutions by the new Law on Service in Armed Forces, or in a different way, to better support reforms for the development of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Croatia, or simply because individual solutions have become inappropriate for military service, which I will later explain on certain examples. The Armed Forces Service is a service of military and professional service through military service, voluntary military training, cadet service, active military service, reserve staff and civil servants' service. I find it extremely important to have a new definition of a working military person as the most numerous categories in the armed forces system that says: "Yes, military personnel, staff are trained to carry out duties and jobs on which they are assigned or appointed, or directed, life- and health-threatening jobs, and at which they are exposed to risks. In which I stress they are unable to choose duties and places of service and are obliged to be available at any time for the performance of duties and jobs." I specifically emphasized this because this definition represents, on the basis of which the bill before you rests. It will give rise to all rights, as well as obligations of persons who have been assigned to a military call, which are governed by these draft laws. As has been prescribed so far, and this law regulates that active military personnel do not have the right to trade union organisation and are not allowed to strike, membership in political parties and running in representative bodies of citizens, local representative bodies and the Croatian Assembly. Although in the Croatian public there is a dispute over this provision of practices over the past 10 years has shown its effectiveness. In accordance with the principles of equal opportunities for equal treatment, members of the armed forces are obliged to respect the dignity of every person, reputation and honor as well as fundamental rights and freedoms of man. A military person who has been injured with dignity, especially if abused in any way, has the right to contact the Committee on Gender Equality or the Committee on the Protection of Military Dignity. Of the other forms of working relationship, or service in the armed forces, I will mention only some of whom I believe there is an interest in the Croatian public. First of all, it's about contracts for candidates for soldiers. The new bill provides for 3 contracts, the first in 3 years, the second in 5 years and the third in 5 years, which is a total of 13 years thinking about when we have passed this provision already now, although it is in the draft law, we have come to the conclusion that it might try to fix it in a way because a 13-year-old professional soldier is generally assigned to a job where there is an unfettered internship for 13 years of his service has a 6.5-year-old work internship, which means that he is 19.5 years old in total. So we were thinking that this provision could be corrected in a way that the soldiers involved in the current contract, that it could be up to 6 years so that they could achieve a retirement period of twenty years and thus, in accordance with the law on the rights of the pension insurance of active military personnel, could be retired under that law and thus ensure minimum protection of their existence after leaving and finding another career. Also, people who have completed high school education meet the conditions for active service can be defined and competed further for cadet service by the end of the calendar year in which they end 22 years of life. Persons who have also achieved the best results in the chosen procedure are referred to on the basis of a training contract for an officer for up to one year, at the first level of officer education and become candidates for officers. If I may give you a brief explanation of what this is about. Individuals who respond to voluntary military service rather than voluntary military service have the possibility of establishing a working relationship means becoming active military personnel in the armed forces. So far, that choice has been radioized means through the selection classification and before being referred to the officer's education, the person would already be based on a working relationship in the armed forces. In practice, it was found that this approach was not good because at the time of the establishment of a working relationship rather than mastering enough knowledge and skills of the need to prove to be persons who have predisposition to be good officers in this law, it was proposed that during the course of their training for officer training they would be contracted which is temporary character for a year, and only if they successfully overcame it will they be accepted into military service. The proposed law also provides for one newspaper, a category of staff who are military specialists, these are persons who possess specialised skills, the need to behave in military duties, in families of combat needs, services and professions, and who are assigned to jobs designated for a military specialist. Military specialists, in addition to specialist knowledge, must meet the general conditions for admission into the active military service, and we have predicted that this provision has a delayed start of application from 1 January 2014 in such a way that from the current structure of staff that no longer has the possibility of progress and has specific skills and does not need their promotion through the acts to be translated into the category of military specialists and thus to remain in the service. Their progress will be further governed by the sublaw of the way that instead of obtaining the rank, they will be carried out through individual paying classes where, for a certain year of life in the service, they will be able to increase the rate of salary calculation. The proposed law is further conceived in several parts, however, because of the scope of the law that you have had the opportunity to read, I would refer only to a few more institutes and solutions that we consider important highlighted because of the very expression of their arrangement or which are a novelty in the law itself. As I have already mentioned in the Defence Act, the President of the Republic shall appoint military commanders on the duty of the Brigadier, General or Admiral, while the Service Act provides that officers, or generals, admirals who, in accordance with the Defence Act, are not appointed by the President of the Republic to office shall be deployed by the Minister of Defence or by the person authorised by him. This law also provides for an institute for a non-permanent member of the armed forces that we have not had so far. As you all know, our members of the armed forces are enlisted in peacekeeping missions and we considered it important and in the law of the service foresees the possibility when a member of the armed forces with whom the commander lost contact during the exercise of combat tasks and participation in operations abroad and could not achieve it within at least 48 hours will be considered temporarily missing. As for the professional development of soldiers, active military personnel, during the service which their professional development takes place through training, training and education, successful conduct of duty and promotion in a higher rank. Professional development standards depend on personal rank, years of life, civil military education, time spent in the service as well as other circumstances important for the performance of the service. Military personnel shall be assigned acts and shall be promoted on the basis of education, training and education, official evaluations of the conduct of duties and performance. The President of the Republic shall, on a proposal from the Minister of Defence, assign the first officer act and promote officers and generals and admirals and the proposal shall be submitted by the Chief of Staff. As regards labour rights, you were able to read and read before the law came to this assembly one of the basic reasons and the amendments to the Service and Armed Forces Act itself were labour rights, and this was precisely the way in which labour rights represented a particular problem and the problem was related to working time, rest and leave because the current law on the service of the armed forces was prescribed to the members of the armed forces to apply the law on the service of the armed forces, rules on civil servants and tenants, general labour regulations, or collective agreements concluded with them. The proposed law leaves this commitment and proposes that the area of labour rights be explicitly regulated by the proposed law and that all the rights that exist in the Labour Act and collective agreements are laid down in this law as we considered most appropriate for military persons. We have tied labour rights to receiving military personnel in such a way that we have established that a normal person has the right to pay and pay for introducing what is called a military supplement and with this military supplement that will receive all the active military personnel depending on the category in which they belong and there will be several categories of military accessories which would exclude the possibility of obtaining all other rights resulting from a collective contract. For example, we had situations where we were unable to engage members of the armed forces for 16 hours because it was overtime, there was a moment when we hired him at night. There was a fee for night work, if it was working on Saturdays, a fee was asked for Saturdays, Sundays, work on holidays. So something we call a military supplement, we will try to make up for it and bring military service into the frameworks that we consider appropriate for the military. We also had another particular problem called an excessive administrative trial on most of the acts in the field of the Service and Armed Forces Act could be launched so that we came up with the situation of an adjudicated administrative trial that we tried to resolve in a way that was prescribed by law which acts are considered administrative matters which solutions are the administrative matter to which the persons involved can file an action, means to the Administrative Court and afterwards to the High Administrative Court, and all other things we have solved by decisions that will not be administrative acts. Armed forces are also responsible and have a strong and material responsibility. The difference between the current Law of Service is that officials and deputies present in the armed forces are exempt from a strict responsibility under the new Law of Service in the Armed Forces. ParlaMint-HR_2013-01-30-0.u28495	Your Honor, Mr. Vice President, Madam Deputy Minister. On behalf of the HDZ Club, let me give a brief review of the proposed Defence Act. So far, the current law, as has already been said, has been passed in the meantime by certain changes about a decade ago. And at that time, as was also said, it was an attempt to adapt the law in which the President of the Republic and the Government shared powers to constitutional changes that marked the beginning of the transformation from half-presidential to parliamentary democracy. Since then, a significant route has been achieved in the defence area, which Croatia has ultimately placed within the collective defence system, which is fundamentally achieved through full membership in the NATO Alliance, and has been further developed for the time being by active observer status, and we are soon hoping for a full participant within the EU's common security and defence policy system. Of course, these changes were necessary to follow the appropriate legislation, which was the process which began intensively to be prepared just over 2 years ago. The document basically represents a step forward compared to the previous law which was expected. The law is well prepared in that section relating to the regulation of the relationship between procedures and competences in international military activities of the armed forces, which was one of the primary objectives of amending the law in the section relating to jurisdiction and procedures and responsibilities at national level with those positive things, we believe that certain improvements are still possible.
